## RippnerLabs/stervault | DeepWiki

# Overview Relevant source files anchor/target/idl/lending.json anchor/target/types/lending.ts package.json src/components/home/home-ui.tsx yarn.lock Stervault is a decentralized finance (DeFi) lending platform built on the Solana blockchain that enables users to deposit assets to earn interest and borrow against collateral.

The platform consists of a Next.js frontend application and a Solana program written using the Anchor framework, integrated with Pyth Network price oracles for real-time asset valuation.

This document provides a high-level overview of the system architecture, core components, and technology stack.

For detailed information about specific subsystems, see Project Structure, Frontend Architecture, Blockchain Architecture, and Core Features. ## System Architecture The Stervault platform follows a three-tier architecture with distinct layers for presentation, data access, and blockchain operations: ### High

and blockchain infrastructure optimized for performance and user experience: | Component | Technology | Version | | --- | --- | --- | | Frontend Framework | Next.js | 14.2.5 | | Blockchain | Solana | | | Wallet Integration | Solana Wallet Adapter | 0.15.35 | | Token Program | SPL Token | 0.4.8 | Sources: package.json21-81 ## Core Lending Program The heart of the platform is the Solana lending program deployed at address FYkahL7zxyc3cS9wwA8b45JUNKoHSw6R4Ln5t7GXU5oD.

The program implements a -based lending and borrowing system with the following key features: ### Program Instructions * initUser: Initializes global user state for new platform users * initBank: Creates new token banks with configurable parameters * deposit: Allows users to deposit tokens and receive interest-bearing s * withdraw: Enables withdrawal of deposited tokens plus accrued interest * borrow: Facilitates borrowing against collateral with LTV checks * repay: Processes loan repayments and collateral releases * initBorrowPosition: Creates new borrowing positions for users * storeSymbolFeedId: Stores Pyth price feed mappings for tokens Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts15-1421 ### Account Structure The program maintains several key account types: 
* Bank: Stores token-specific lending parameters and aggregate statistics 
* UserGlobalState: Tracks user's overall platform activity and positions 
* UserTokenState: Manages user balances for specific tokens 
* BorrowPosition: Represents individual borrowing positions with collateral 
* PythNetworkFeedId: Maps token symbols to Pyth price feed identifiers Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts1590-1939 ## Price Oracle Integration The platform integrates with Pyth Network for real-time price feeds, essential for accurate collateral valuation and risk management: ``` #mermaid-owdrvf58li{font-family:ui-sans-serif,-apple-system,system-ui,Segoe freshness using a maximum age constant of 10,000 seconds and integrates price data into borrowing and liquidation calculations.

Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts1942-1947 package.json26-27 ## Frontend Application The frontend is built as a modern React application using Next.js, providing users with an intuitive interface for interacting with the lending protocol. ### Key components include: 
* Market Interface: Displays available lending markets and current rates 
* Deposit/Withdrawal Forms: Transaction interfaces for depositing and withdrawing assets 
* Borrowing Interface: Tools for managing collateralized borrowing positions 
* Transaction History: Comprehensive activity tracking and reporting 
* Wallet Integration: Seamless connection to Solana wallets The application uses React Query for efficient data fetching and caching, with custom hooks abstracting blockchain interactions.

Sources: src/components/home/home-ui.tsx30-66 package.json46-47 ## Deployment and Infrastructure The platform supports multiple deployment environments: 
* Local Development: Solana localnet with token faucets and price feed simulation 
* Devnet: Solana devnet for testing with live price feeds 
* Mainnet: Production deployment on Solana mainnet 
* AWS Infrastructure: Frontend hosting and content delivery Build and deployment scripts are provided for each environment, with environment-specific configurations managed through .env files.

Sources: package.json5-19 This overview establishes the foundation for understanding Stervault's architecture and capabilities.

For detailed implementation specifics, refer to the specialized documentation sections covering each major system component.

ProgramSystem ProgramAssociated Token Program ``` Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts15-1421 anchor/target/idl/lending.json9-1415 ## Technology Stack The platform leverages modern web technologies and blockchain infrastructure optimized for performance and user experience: | Component | Technology | Version | | --- | --- | --- | | Frontend Framework | Next.js | 14.2.5 | | Blockchain | Solana | - | | Smart Contract Framework | Anchor | 0.30.1 | | Price Oracles | Pyth Network | 1.9.0 | | UI Library | React | 18.x | | Styling | Tailwind CSS | 3.4.1 | | State Management | React Query | 5.51.11 | | Wallet Integration | Solana Wallet Adapter | 0.15.35 | | Token Program | SPL Token | 0.4.8 | Sources: package.json21-81 ## Core Lending Program The heart of the platform is the Solana lending program deployed at address FYkahL7zxyc3cS9wwA8b45JUNKoHSw6R4Ln5t7GXU5oD.

The program implements a -based lending and borrowing system with the following key features: ### Program Instructions * initUser: Initializes global user state for new platform users * initBank: Creates new token banks with configurable parameters * deposit: Allows users to deposit tokens and receive interest-bearing s * withdraw: Enables withdrawal of deposited tokens plus accrued interest * borrow: Facilitates borrowing against collateral with LTV checks * repay: Processes loan repayments and collateral releases * initBorrowPosition: Creates new borrowing positions for users * storeSymbolFeedId: Stores Pyth price feed mappings for tokens Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts15-1421 ### Account Structure The program maintains several key account types: 
* Bank: Stores token-specific lending parameters and aggregate statistics 
* UserGlobalState: Tracks user's overall platform activity and positions 
* UserTokenState: Manages user balances for specific tokens 
* BorrowPosition: Represents individual borrowing positions with collateral 
* PythNetworkFeedId: Maps token symbols to Pyth price feed identifiers Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts1590-1939 ## Price Oracle Integration The platform integrates with Pyth Network for real-time price feeds, essential for accurate collateral valuation and risk management: ``` #mermaid-q8sntdrp71{font-family:ui-sans-serif,-apple-system,system-ui,Segoe freshness using a maximum age constant of 10,000 seconds and integrates price data into borrowing and liquidation calculations.

Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts1942-1947 package.json26-27 ## Frontend Application The frontend is built as a modern React application using Next.js, providing users with an intuitive interface for interacting with the lending protocol. ### Key components include: 
* Market Interface: Displays available lending markets and current rates 
* Deposit/Withdrawal Forms: Transaction interfaces for depositing and withdrawing assets 
* Borrowing Interface: Tools for managing collateralized borrowing positions 
* Transaction History: Comprehensive activity tracking and reporting 
* Wallet Integration: Seamless connection to Solana wallets The application uses React Query for efficient data fetching and caching, with custom hooks abstracting blockchain interactions.

Sources: src/components/home/home-ui.tsx30-66 package.json46-47 ## Deployment and Infrastructure The platform supports multiple deployment environments: 
* Local Development: Solana localnet with token faucets and price feed simulation 
* Devnet: Solana devnet for testing with live price feeds 
* Mainnet: Production deployment on Solana mainnet 
* AWS Infrastructure: Frontend hosting and content delivery Build and deployment scripts are provided for each environment, with environment-specific configurations managed through .env files.

Sources: package.json5-19 This overview establishes the foundation for understanding Stervault's architecture and capabilities.

For detailed implementation specifics, refer to the specialized documentation sections covering each major system component.

---

## Application Layout and Navigation

# Application Layout and Navigation Relevant source files src/components/home/home-ui.tsx src/components/sidebar/sidebar-ui.tsx This document covers the frontend application layout structure, navigation systems, and routing patterns used throughout the Stervault DeFi lending platform.

It focuses on the main application layout, sidebar navigation, breadcrumb system, and responsive navigation behaviors.

For information about the individual UI components used within these layouts, see UI Component System.

For details about how navigation triggers data fetching and state updates, see Data Access Layer. ## Overall Layout Architecture The application uses two distinct layout patterns: a marketing landing ## Project Structure

# Project Structure Relevant source files anchor/target/idl/lending.json anchor/target/types/lending.ts package.json yarn.lock This document provides an overview of the Stervault codebase organization, covering the main directories, key files, and architectural patterns.

It explains how the frontend Next.js application connects to the Solana lending program and supporting infrastructure.

For detailed information about the lending program's instructions and accounts, see Program IDL Reference.

For frontend component architecture, see UI Component System. ## High 
- Level Architecture Stervault is organized as a full-stack DeFi lending platform with distinct layers for frontend presentation, blockchain interaction, and deployment infrastructure. ``` ## Frontend Structure The frontend is built with Next.js and follows a modern React architecture with TypeScript support.

The application uses a component-based structure with data access hooks for blockchain interaction. ### Key Frontend Technologies | Technology | Version | Purpose | | --- | --- | --- | | next | 14.2.5 | React framework and routing | | @tanstack/react-query | ^5.51.11 | Data fetching and caching | | @radix-ui/* | Various | UI component primitives | | tailwindcss | ^3.4.1 | Styling framework | | Blockchain Layer The blockchain layer is implemented as a Solana program using the Anchor framework.

The program defines a comprehensive lending protocol with support for deposits, borrowing, and collateralized positions. ### Core Program Instructions ``` Flow Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts15-1422 anchor/target/idl/lending.json9-1416 ### Program Account Structure The lending program manages several key account types that store user and system state: | Account Type | Purpose | Key Fields | | --- | --- | --- | | Bank | Token pool management | totalDepositeds, totalBorroweds, liquidationThreshold | | UserGlobalState | User's global lending state | depositedMints, activePositions, positions | | UserTokenState | Per-token user state | depositeds, collaterals, borroweds | | BorrowPosition | Individual borrow position | collaterals, borroweds, active | | PythNetworkFeedId | Price feed mapping | symbol, feedId | Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts1423-1502 anchor/target/idl/lending.json1417-1496 ## Development Scripts and Configuration The project includes comprehensive build and deployment automation through npm scripts defined in package.json. ### Build and Development Scripts ``` Environment Configuration The project supports multiple deployment environments through environment-specific configurations: 
* Local Development: Standard Next.js development server 
* Devnet: Solana devnet with env-cmd -f .env.devnet 
* Custom: Custom environment with env-cmd -f .env.custom * *
* 

**AWS:**

Dependencies The frontend leverages modern React patterns with comprehensive UI component libraries: 
* State Management: @tanstack/react-query for server state, jotai for client state 
* UI Components: Radix UI primitives with Tailwind CSS styling 
* Animation: Framer Motion for smooth transitions 
* Forms: React Hook Form with Zod validation 
* Development: TypeScript, ESLint, and Prettier for code quality Sources: package.json21-82 package.json83-103 This project structure enables a clean separation of concerns between the frontend application and the Solana program while providing robust tooling for development and deployment across different environments.

---

## Key Technologies

# Key Technologies Relevant source files anchor/bun.lock anchor/price-feeds/pyth_mapping_devnet.json anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/deposit.rs package.json src/app/deposit-tokens/layout.tsx yarn.lock ## Purpose and Scope This document provides a comprehensive overview of the technology stack used in the Stervault DeFi lending platform, covering frontend frameworks, blockchain infrastructure, price oracles, and supporting development tools.

The platform combines modern web technologies with Solana blockchain capabilities to deliver a decentralized lending protocol.

For information about the overall project structure and file organization, see Project Structure.

For detailed implementation patterns and component architecture, see Frontend Architecture.

For blockchain program specifics, see Blockchain Architecture. ## Core Technology Stack Overview The platform is built on a modern, decentralized architecture combining Next.js for the frontend with Solana for blockchain functionality.

- Jest ``` Sources: package.json1-104 ## Frontend Technologies ### Next.js Framework The application uses Next.js 14.2.5 as the primary React framework, providing server-side rendering, routing, and build optimization.

The framework supports both development and production environments with environment-specific configurations.

## Frontend Architecture

# Frontend Architecture Relevant source files package.json public/tokens_devnet.json src/app/api/faucet/route.ts src/components/cluster/cluster-data-access.tsx src/components/sidebar/sidebar-ui.tsx yarn.lock This document provides a comprehensive overview of the Stervault frontend architecture, built as a React/Next.js application for interacting with the Solana-based lending protocol.

It covers the application structure, core technologies, navigation system, data access patterns, and UI component organization.

For information about the underlying Solana lending program, see Blockchain Architecture.

For details about specific user features, see Core Features. ## Application Structure Overview The frontend is built as a Next.js 14 application using the App Router pattern, providing a modern React-based interface for the Solana lending platform.

The application follows a component-driven architecture with clear separation between UI presentation, data access, and business logic. ``` Sources: src/components/cluster/cluster-data-access.tsx9-21 src/components/cluster/cluster-data-access.tsx26-37 src/components/cluster/cluster-data-access.tsx39-55 src/components/cluster/cluster-data-access.tsx68-91 ### Data Flow Patterns The application follows a consistent pattern for blockchain data access: 
* Provider Layer: Context providers manage global state (cluster, wallet) 
* Hook Layer: Custom hooks encapsulate data fetching logic 
* Component Layer: React components consume hooks for presentation 
* Caching Layer: React Query manages server state and caching ``` #mermaid-ckgryxuahrs{font-family:ui-sans-serif,-apple-system,system-ui,Segoe UI,Helvetica;font-size:16px;fill:#333;}@keyframes Sources: src/app/api/faucet/route.ts42-79 src/app/api/faucet/route.ts82-97 src/app/api/faucet/route.ts100-159 src/app/api/faucet/route.ts162-266 ## Component System and UI Patterns The application uses a component-driven architecture with Radix UI primitives as the foundation for consistent, accessible interfaces. ### Core UI Components The component system is built on Radix UI primitives, providing: 
* Accessibility: ARIA-compliant components out of the box 
* Customization: Styled with Tailwind CSS classes 
* Consistency: d design tokens and patterns 
* Modularity: Composable component architecture Key component categories include: | Component Type | Examples | Purpose | | --- | --- | --- | | Layout | Sidebar, SidebarBody, SidebarLink | Application shell and navigation | | Navigation | Breadcrumb, BreadcrumbItem | Page hierarchy and wayfinding | | Interactive | Popover, PopoverContent, Button | User interactions and dialogs | | Data Display | Tables, Cards, Status indicators | Information presentation | | Form Controls | Inputs, Selects, Sliders | User input and form handling | Sources: src/components/sidebar/sidebar-ui.tsx4-19 src/components/sidebar/sidebar-ui.tsx25-36 package.json28-37 This architecture provides a scalable foundation for the lending platform interface while maintaining consistency with modern web accessibility standards and React patterns.

## UI Component System

# UI Component System Relevant source files src/app/page.tsx src/components/ui/avatar.tsx src/components/ui/features-section-demo-3.tsx src/components/ui/footer.tsx src/components/ui/layout-grid-demo.tsx src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx src/components/ui/timeline-demo.tsx src/components/ui/timeline.tsx This document covers the reusable UI component system that forms the foundation of the Stervault frontend interface.

The component system provides consistent styling, animations, and user interactions across the application using a layered architecture built on React, TypeScript, and modern UI libraries.

For information about how these components integrate with blockchain data, see Data Access Layer.

For application-level layout and navigation patterns, see Application Layout and Navigation. ## Component Architecture Overview The UI component system follows a three-tier architecture with base src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx7-12 src/components/ui/avatar.tsx8-20 ## Base Component System ### Radix UI Integration The component system builds upon Radix UI primitives to provide accessible, unstyled components that are then enhanced with Tailwind CSS styling and Framer Motion animations.

The Avatar component demonstrates this pattern: | Component Layer | Implementation | Purpose | | --- | --- | --- | | AvatarPrimitive.

Root | Radix UI primitive | Accessibility and behavior | | Avatar | Wrapped primitive | Custom styling with Tailwind | | AvatarImage | Image primitive wrapper | Styled image display | | AvatarFallback | Fallback primitive wrapper | Styled placeholder content | The Avatar component uses React's forwardRef pattern for proper ref forwarding and applies consistent styling through the cn utility function that combines class names.

Sources: src/components/ui/avatar.tsx8-48 ### Component Props and TypeScript Integration Components use strict TypeScript interfaces to ensure type safety: ``` // Timeline component interface interface TimelineEntry { title: string; content: React.

ReactNode; } // Layout grid card interface type Card = { id: number; content: JSX.

Element | React.

ReactNode | string; className: string; thumbnail: string; }; ``` Sources: src/components/ui/timeline.tsx10-13 src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx7-12 ## Composite Components ### Timeline Component The Timeline component creates scroll-triggered animated timelines using Framer Motion's scroll progress tracking.

Key features include: 
* Scroll Progress Tracking: Uses useScroll hook with offset configuration src/components/ui/timeline.tsx27-30 
* Dynamic Height Calculation: Measures container height for progress bar scaling src/components/ui/timeline.tsx20-25 
* Smooth Animations: Transforms scroll progress into height and opacity values src/components/ui/timeline.tsx32-33 The component renders timeline entries with sticky positioning and animated progress indicators.

Sources: src/components/ui/timeline.tsx15-90 ### Layout Grid Component The LayoutGrid component provides an interactive card grid with modal-style expansion: 
* State Management: Tracks selected and last selected cards src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx15-16 
* Click Handling: Manages card selection and outside click detection src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx18-26 
* Layout Animations: Uses Framer Motion's layoutId for smooth transitions src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx43 
* Overlay System: Implements backdrop overlay with opacity animations src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx50-57 Sources: src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx14-113 ### Footer Component The Footer component provides site-wide navigation and branding: 
* Responsive Grid: Uses CSS Grid for adaptive layout src/components/ui/footer.tsx30 
* Link Organization: Groups links by category (Platform, Social, Resources, Legal) src/components/ui/footer.tsx31-202 
* Brand Integration: Includes logo and company information src/components/ui/footer.tsx6-28 Sources: src/components/ui/footer.tsx1-211 ## Animation and Motion System ### Framer Motion Integration Components leverage Framer Motion for sophisticated animations: | Animation Type | Component | Implementation | | --- | --- | --- | | Scroll-triggered | Timeline | useScroll + useTransform | | Layout transitions | LayoutGrid | layoutId + AnimatePresence | | Hover effects | FeaturesSectionDemo | whileHover + whileTap variants | | Entrance animations | SelectedCard | Initial, animate, exit transitions | ### Animation Patterns Scroll Progress Animation: ``` const { scrollYProgress } = useScroll({ target: containerRef, offset: ["start 10%", "end 50%"], }); const heightTransform = useTransform(scrollYProgress, [0, 1], [0, height]); ``` Layout ID Transitions: ``` <motion.div layoutId={`card-${card.id}`}> {/ 
* Card content */} </motion.div> ``` Sources: src/components/ui/timeline.tsx27-33 src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx43 src/components/ui/features-section-demo-3.tsx160-171 ## Demo Components and Usage Patterns ### Timeline Demo Implementation The TimelineDemo component demonstrates real-world Timeline usage for user onboarding flows: 
* Content Structure: Each step includes title, description, images, and feature checkmarks src/components/ui/timeline-demo.tsx6-167 
* Visual Design: Uses responsive image grids and feature lists src/components/ui/timeline-demo.tsx14-29 
* User Journey Mapping: Follows Connect → Deposit → Lend/Borrow → Withdraw flow ### Layout Grid Demo Implementation The LayoutGridDemo displays market statistics using card-based layouts: 
* Data Visualization: TVL, APY rates, top pools, and transaction data src/components/ui/layout-grid-demo.tsx19-86 
* Dynamic Content: Real-time market data presentation with styled cards src/components/ui/layout-grid-demo.tsx88-116 
* Responsive Design: Adaptive grid columns based on screen size ### Features Section Demo The FeaturesSectionDemo showcases platform capabilities with interactive elements: 
* Feature Cards: Structured feature presentation with skeleton loading states src/components/ui/features-section-demo-3.tsx11-42 
* Interactive Elements: Globe animation, image galleries, and video links src/components/ui/features-section-demo-3.tsx226-299 
* Educational Content: Links to external resources and documentation Sources: src/components/ui/timeline-demo.tsx5-172 src/components/ui/layout-grid-demo.tsx5-117 src/components/ui/features-section-demo-3.tsx10-300 ## Styling and Theming System ### Tailwind CSS Integration The component system uses Tailwind CSS with a consistent design token approach: | Design Token | Usage | Examples | | --- | --- | --- | | Colors | text-foreground, text-muted-foreground | Semantic color naming | | Spacing | p-4 sm:p-8, gap-10 | Responsive spacing scale | | Layout | grid grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-6 | Responsive grid systems | | Typography | text-xl md:text-2xl | Responsive text scaling | ### Theme Consistency Components maintain visual consistency through: 
* Semantic Color Classes: text-foreground, bg-background, border-border 
* Responsive Breakpoints: Consistent sm:, md:, lg: usage 
* Shadow System: Standardized shadow utilities for depth 
* Border Radius: Consistent rounded-xl, rounded-lg usage Sources: src/components/ui/timeline.tsx37-85 src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx29-59 src/components/ui/footer.tsx3-205 ## Component Patterns and Conventions ### File Organization Components follow a consistent file structure: 
* Base Components: src/components/ui/[component].tsx 
* Demo Components: src/components/ui/[component]-demo.tsx 
* Naming Convention: PascalCase for components, kebab-case for files ### Development Patterns 
* TypeScript First: All components use strict TypeScript interfaces 
* Accessibility: Built on accessible Radix UI primitives where applicable 
* Performance: Uses React best practices including forwardRef and proper dependency arrays 
* Composability: Components accept children and support custom className props Sources: src/components/ui/avatar.tsx1-51 src/components/ui/timeline.tsx1-91 src/components/ui/layout-grid.tsx1-114

## Data Access Layer

# Data Access Layer Relevant source files public/tokens_devnet.json src/app/api/faucet/route.ts src/app/layout.tsx src/components/cluster/cluster-data-access.tsx src/components/dashboard/dashboard-data-access.tsx src/components/dashboard/dashboard-ui.tsx src/components/faucet/faucet-data-access.tsx src/components/faucet/faucet-ui.tsx src/lib/connection-health.ts src/lib/request-batcher.ts ## Purpose and Scope The Data Access Layer provides a unified interface for fetching, caching, and managing blockchain data in the Stervault application.

This layer abstracts the complexity of interacting with Solana RPC endpoints, Pyth price feeds, and internal APIs while providing robust error handling, caching, and performance optimizations.

This document covers the React-based data access patterns, custom hooks, providers, and utilities.

For information about the underlying Solana program interactions, see Blockchain Architecture.

For UI component patterns that consume this data, see UI Component System. ## Architecture Overview The data access layer is built on a foundation of React Query for server state management, Jotai for client-side atomic state, and custom hooks that encapsulate complex data fetching logic. ### Data Access Architecture ``` ``` Sources: src/app/layout.tsx2-29 ## Blockchain Architecture

# Blockchain Architecture Relevant source files anchor/anchor/keys/user.json anchor/programs/lending/src/error.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/init_borrow_position.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/lib.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs anchor/target/idl/lending.json anchor/target/types/lending.ts src/app/api/onboarding/route.ts This document covers the Solana-based lending program that forms the core blockchain component of the Stervault platform.

It details the program's account structures, instructions, state management, and integration with external services like Pyth Network price oracles.

For information about how the frontend interacts with this blockchain program, see Frontend Architecture.

For details about the specific lending and borrowing features, see Core Features. ## Program Overview The Stervault lending program is deployed on Solana at program ID FYkahL7zxyc3cS9wwA8b45JUNKoHSw6R4Ln5t7GXU5oD.

It implements a decentralized lending protocol where users can deposit assets, use them as collateral, and borrow against their collateral positions. ### Core Program Flow ``` max_ltv | Manages liquidity pool for each token mint | | UserTokenState | deposited_s, collateral_s, borrowed_s | Tracks user's position in specific token | | UserGlobalState | deposited_mints, active_positions, positions | Global user state across all tokens | | BorrowPosition | collateral_s, borrowed_s, position_id | Individual borrow position with collateral | | PythNetworkFeedId | tokens | | repay | Both banks, BorrowPosition, price feeds, token accounts | Repay borrowed amount, update position | | withdraw | Bank, UserTokenState, token accounts | Burn s, transfer tokens to anchor/programs/lending/src/error.rs1-40 ### Critical Error Codes | Error Code | Trigger Condition | Impact | | --- | --- | --- | | 6000 | BorrowAmountTooLarge | Prevents over-borrowing against collateral | | 6001 | WithdrawAmountExceedsCollateralValue | Maintains collateralization ratio | | 6008 | InvalidPriceFeed | Ensures price data integrity | | 6012 | StalePrice | Prevents operations with outdated prices | | 6015 | InsufficientCollateral | Protects protocol from under-collateralized positions | Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/error.rs4-38 ## Program Constants and Configuration The program uses several constants to control system behavior and limits. ### Key Configuration Values 
* Program *
* 

**ID:**
*
* FYkahL7zxyc3cS9wwA8b45JUNKoHSw6R4Ln5t7GXU5oD 
* Maximum Mints: Defined in MAX_MINTS constant 
* Maximum Borrow Positions: Defined in MAX_BORROW_POSITIONS constant 
* Treasury Seed: "treasury" for bank token account PDAs 
* Position Seed: "position" for borrow position PDAs 
* User Global Seed: "user_global" for user global state PDAs Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts8 anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs2 anchor/programs/lending/src/lib.rs9 ### Bank Configuration Parameters Each Bank account stores configurable risk parameters: * liquidation_threshold: Maximum LTV before liquidation * liquidation_bonus: Liquidator reward percentage * max_ltv: Maximum loan-to-value ratio * deposit_interest_rate / borrow_interest_rate: APY rates * deposit_fee / withdrawal_fee: Transaction fees * min_deposit: Minimum deposit amount Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs18-40 anchor/target/types/lending.ts598-653

## Program Instructions

# Program Instructions Relevant source files anchor/bun.lock anchor/price-feeds/pyth_mapping_devnet.json anchor/programs/lending/src/constants.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/borrow.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/deposit.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/init_user.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/init_user_token_state.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/mod.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/repay.rs src/app/deposit-tokens/layout.tsx This document provides comprehensive documentation of all Solana program instructions available in the Stervault lending protocol.

These instructions handle user onboarding, asset deposits/withdrawals, borrowing operations, and system configuration.

For information about the account structures these instructions operate on, see Account Structure and State Management.

For details about price oracle integration used by borrowing instructions, see Price Oracle Integration. ## Instruction Overview The lending program provides nine core instructions that manage the complete lifecycle of a decentralized lending protocol: | Instruction | Purpose | Key Accounts | | --- | --- | --- | | init_user | Initialize global user state | UserGlobalState | | init_user_token_state | Initialize user state for specific token | UserTokenState, UserGlobalState | | init_bank | Create new lending bank for token | Bank | | deposit | Deposit assets into lending bank | Bank, UserTokenState | | withdraw | Withdraw deposited assets | Bank, UserTokenState | | borrow | Borrow against collateral | Bank, BorrowPosition, price oracles | | repay | Repay borrowed assets | Bank, BorrowPosition, price oracles | | store_symbol_feed_id | Configure price feed mappings | PythNetworkFeedId | | init_borrow_position | Initialize borrowing position | BorrowPosition | Initialization Instructions ### init_user Initializes the global user state account that tracks user's overall lending activity across all tokens.

Account Structure: * signer: User wallet (mutable, signer) * user_global_state: PDA account storing global user data * system_program: Solana system program Process: 
* Creates UserGlobalState account using PDA seed ["user_global", signer.key()] 
* Initializes empty deposited_mints vector 
* Sets user pubkey and bump seed Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/init_user.rs4-27 ### init_user_token_state Initializes user state for a specific token mint, enabling deposits and borrowing for that token.

Account Structure: * signer: User wallet (mutable, signer) * user_account: PDA for user's token-specific state * user_global_state: Global user state account * system_program: Solana system program Process: 
* Creates UserTokenState using PDA seed [signer.key(), mint_address.key()] 
* Initializes timestamps for interest calculations 
* Updates global state with new mint Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/init_user_token_state.rs4-46 ## Core Lending Instructions ### deposit Transfers user tokens into a lending bank * Interest accrual before calculations using accrue_interest() 
* Proportional minting based on existing bank state 
* Global state tracking of deposited mints Calculation: 
* First deposit: s = amount 
* Subsequent: s = (amount * total_s) / total_assets Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/deposit.rs9-218 ### borrow Creates a leveraged borrowing position by locking collateral and ValidationLiquidity CheckCollateral CheckPyth Borrow PricePyth Collateral PricePrice ValidationLock Collateral sMint Borrow sTransfer Borrow TokensCreate BorrowPosition ``` Critical Validations: 
* LTV Check: total_debt_value <= collateral_value * max_ltv / 10000 
* Liquidity Check: amount <= available_liquidity 
* Collateral Sufficiency: User has enough deposited s to lock Account Requirements: 
* Dual bank accounts (borrow + collateral tokens) 
* Pyth price feeds for both tokens 
* Treasury token accounts with PDA authority 
* User global state for position tracking Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/borrow.rs12-395 ### repay Repays borrowed assets and unlocks proportional collateral s.

Key Process: * -to 
- Token Conversion: Converts borrow s to token amount 
* USD Value Calculation: Uses Pyth oracles for collateral unlock calculation 
* Proportional Unlock: collateral_unlock = repay_usd_value / collateral_token_price 
* State Cleanup: Deactivates position if fully repaid Position Management: 
* Tracks positions in UserGlobalState.active_positions 
* Removes from active list when borrowed_s = 0 
* Updates BorrowPosition.active = false for closed positions Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/repay.rs10-317 ## Administrative Instructions ### init_bank Creates a new lending bank for a specific token mint with configured interest rates and risk parameters. ### store_symbol_feed_id Maps token symbols to Pyth Network price feed IDs for oracle price lookups. ### init_borrow_position Pre-initializes a borrowing position account before executing borrow instruction.

Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/mod.rs5-20 ## Account Validation Patterns All instructions follow consistent PDA validation patterns: ``` * All PDAs use deterministic seeds for account derivation 
* Treasury accounts use program-derived authorities 
* Oracle accounts validated against stored feed IDs 
* Bump seeds stored and validated for PDA security Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/borrow.rs21-115 anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/repay.rs19-111 anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/deposit.rs16-58

## Lending Program Overview

# Lending Program Overview Relevant source files anchor/anchor/keys/user.json anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/init_borrow_position.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/lib.rs anchor/target/idl/lending.json anchor/target/types/lending.ts src/app/api/onboarding/route.ts ## Purpose and Scope This document provides a high-level overview of the Solana-based lending program that forms the core blockchain infrastructure of the Stervault DeFi platform.

The lending program enables users to deposit assets, borrow against collateral, and manage lending positions through a decentralized protocol deployed at program address FYkahL7zxyc3cS9wwA8b45JUNKoHSw6R4Ln5t7GXU5oD.

For detailed information about specific program instructions, see Program Instructions.

For account structure details, see Account Structure and State Management.

For price oracle integration specifics, see Price Oracle Integration. ## Core Program Structure The lending program is built using the Anchor framework and provides eight primary instructions that handle the complete lending lifecycle.

The program architecture follows a bank-based model where each supported token has its own Bank account that manages deposits, borrowing parameters, and liquidity. ``` protocol state: | Account Type | Purpose | PDA Seeds | Key Fields | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | Bank | Manages token-specific lending parameters and liquidity | [mint_address] | total_deposited_s, total_borrowed_s, deposit_interest_rate, borrow_interest_rate | | UserGlobalState | Tracks user's overall protocol participation | ["user_global", signer] | Global user metadata and counters | | UserTokenState | Manages user's position in specific token bank | [signer, mint_address] | User's deposit and collateral s for specific token | | BorrowPosition | Represents individual borrowing arrangement | ["position", signer, mint_collateral, mint_borrow, position_id] | Collateral and borrow amounts for specific position | | PythNetworkFeedId | Maps token symbols to Pyth price feed IDs | ["pyth_network_feed_id", symbol] | Price feed identifiers for oracle integration | Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts1423-1501 anchor/target/idl/lending.json1321-1399 ## Main Instructions Flow liquiditywithdraw(amount)Validate available balanceReduce total_deposited_s ``` Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/lib.rs20-77 anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/init_borrow_position.rs5-34 ## Risk Management and Price Integration The lending program implements comprehensive risk management through integration with Pyth Network price oracles and configurable risk parameters per token bank: ### Risk Parameters Each Bank account stores critical risk management parameters configured during initialization: * liquidation_threshold: The collateral ratio below which positions become liquidatable * liquidation_bonus: Additional reward for liquidators * max_ltv: Maximum loan-to-value ratio for new borrows * liquidation_close_factor: Maximum portion of position that can be liquidated ### Price Oracle Integration The program requires real-time price data for both collateral and borrow tokens in all borrowing and repayment operations.

Price feeds are accessed through: * price_update_borrow_token and price_update_collateral_token accounts * pyth_network_feed_id_borrow_token and pyth_network_feed_id_collateral_token accounts 
* Symbol-to-feed 
- ID mapping stored in PythNetworkFeedId accounts Sources: anchor/target/types/lending.ts604-653 anchor/target/types/lending.ts298-307 ## Program Architecture Overview ``` - Token)BorrowPosition(Per Position)PythNetworkFeedId(Per Symbol)Deposit ProcessingBorrow ProcessingRepay ProcessingWithdraw ProcessingRisk CalculationsBank Treasury AccountsUser Associated Token Accounts ``` Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/lib.rs1-83 anchor/target/types/lending.ts7-14 The lending program serves as the foundational blockchain infrastructure that enables all DeFi lending operations in the Stervault platform.

It provides a secure, decentralized protocol for asset management while maintaining compatibility with standard Solana token operations and integrating real-time price data for accurate risk assessment.

---

## Account Structure and State Management

# Account Structure and State Management Relevant source files anchor/programs/lending/src/error.rs anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs anchor/target/idl/lending.json anchor/target/types/lending.ts This document covers the account structure and state management patterns used in the Stervault lending program.

It explains the different account types, their Program Derived Address (PDA) derivation patterns, data structures, and how state is managed across user interactions and lending operations.

For information about the specific program instructions that operate on these accounts, see Program Instructions.

For details about price oracle integration that uses some of these accounts, see Price Oracle Integration. ## Account Types Overview The lending program manages five primary account types, each serving a specific role in the lending protocol: | Account Type | Purpose | Creation Trigger | | --- | --- | --- | | Bank | Stores lending pool configuration and aggregate state | Administrator creates new lending market | | UserGlobalState | Tracks user's overall state across all tokens | First user interaction with protocol | | UserTokenState | Manages user's position for a specific token | User deposits/borrows specific token | | BorrowPosition | Represents a specific collateralized borrow | User initiates borrow with collateral | | PythNetworkFeedId | Maps token symbols to Pyth price feed IDs | Administrator configures price feeds | Account Architecture Diagram ``` #mermaid-zwuy30qoq1p{font-family:ui-sans-serif,-apple-system,system-ui,Segoe anchor/target/idl/lending.json798-815 anchor/target/idl/lending.json670-702 anchor/target/idl/lending.json1216-1248 ## Account Data Structures ### Bank Account Structure The Bank account stores configuration and aggregate state for each lending market: | Field | Type | Purpose | | --- | --- | --- | | authority | Pubkey | Account authorized to modify bank parameters | | mint_address | Pubkey | SPL token mint for this lending market | | total_deposited_s | u64 | Total s representing all deposits | | total_collateral_s | u64 | Total s used as collateral | | total_borrowed_s | u64 | Total s representing all borrows | | deposit_interest_rate | u64 | Annual deposit interest rate (basis points) | | borrow_interest_rate | u64 | Annual borrow interest rate (basis points) | | last_compound_time | i64 | Timestamp of last interest compounding | | liquidation_threshold | u64 | Threshold for liquidation (basis points) | | max_ltv | u64 | Maximum loan-to-value ratio (basis points) | Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs16-40 anchor/target/idl/lending.json1585-1663 ### UserGlobalState Account Structure The UserGlobalState tracks a user's overall interaction with the protocol: | Field | Type | Purpose | | --- | --- | --- | | user | Pubkey | Owner of this global state account | | deposited_mints | Vec<Pubkey> | List of tokens user has deposited | | active_positions | Vec<Pubkey> | List of active borrow position accounts | | positions | u64 | Counter for position ID generation | | bump | u8 | PDA bump seed for account derivation | Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs51-61 anchor/target/idl/lending.json1830-1859 ### UserTokenState Account Structure The UserTokenState manages a user's position for a specific token: | Field | Type | Purpose | | --- | --- | --- | | owner | Pubkey | User who owns this token state | | mint_address | Pubkey | Token mint this state tracks | | deposited_s | u64 | User's of deposits in this token | | collateral_s | u64 | User's used as collateral | | borrowed_s | u64 | User's of borrows for this token | | last_updated_deposited | i64 | Last update timestamp for deposits | | last_updated_borrowed | i64 | Last update timestamp for borrows | | last_updated_collateral | i64 | Last update timestamp for collateral | Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs3-14 anchor/target/idl/lending.json1862-1899 ### BorrowPosition Account Structure The BorrowPosition represents a specific collateralized borrow: | Field | Type | Purpose | | --- | --- | --- | | position_id | u64 | Unique identifier for this position | | owner | Pubkey | User who owns this borrow position | | collateral_mint | Pubkey | Token used as collateral | | borrow_mint | Pubkey | Token being borrowed | | collateral_s | u64 | Amount of collateral (in s) | | borrowed_s | u64 | Amount borrowed (in s) | | last_updated | i64 | Last update timestamp | | active | bool | Whether position is currently active | Sources: anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs63-74 anchor/target/idl/lending.json1666-1703 ## State Management Patterns ### Account Initialization Flow The program follows a specific initialization sequence for user accounts: Account Initialization Sequence ``` anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs8-10 anchor/programs/lending/src/state.rs70-71 ### State Update Patterns State updates follow consistent patterns across all account types: 
* Timestamp Tracking: All user accounts maintain last_updated timestamps for interest calculations 
* Aggregate Updates: Bank accounts update total s when user positions change 
* Cross 
- Account References: UserGlobalState maintains lists of related accounts for efficient querying 
* Position Lifecycle: Borrow positions track active status and can be deactivated when fully repaid State Update Flow ``` #mermaid-9ivn39vmnu{font-family:ui-sans-serif,-apple-system,system-ui,Segoe ## Price Oracle Integration

# Price Oracle Integration Relevant source files anchor/bun.lock anchor/price-feeds/pyth_mapping_devnet.json anchor/programs/lending/src/instructions/deposit.rs public/tokens_localnet.json src/app/deposit-tokens/layout.tsx This document covers the integration of Pyth Network price oracles within the Stervault lending platform.

Price oracles provide real-time asset pricing data essential for risk management, collateral valuation, and interest calculations across all lending operations.

For information about the core lending program functionality, see Lending Program Overview.

For details on risk management and LTV calculations that depend on oracle prices, see Account Structure and State Management. ## Pyth Network Integration Overview The platform integrates with Pyth Network to obtain reliable, decentralized price feeds for supported assets.

Pyth Network provides high-frequency price updates with cryptographic proofs, ensuring price data integrity for lending operations.

The integration uses two primary Pyth Network dependencies: * @pythnetwork/price-service-client for price data retrieval * @pythnetwork/pyth-solana-receiver for on-chain price feed processing ``` operations through asset valuation and risk management functions. ### Asset Valuation in Deposits The deposit instruction references price feeds for accurate asset valuation during calculations: ``` ## Core Features

# Core Features Relevant source files src/app/withdraw/layout.tsx src/app/withdraw/page.tsx src/components/add-bank/add-bank-data-access.tsx src/components/borrow/active-borrow-positions.tsx src/components/deposit-tokens/deposit-tokens-ui.tsx src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx src/components/deposits/deposits-ui.tsx src/components/markets/markets-data-access.tsx src/components/repay/repay-ui.tsx src/components/withdraw/withdraw-data-access.tsx src/components/withdraw/withdraw-ui.tsx This document provides a comprehensive overview of the core user-facing features of the Stervault DeFi lending platform and their technical implementation.

These features enable users to deposit tokens, earn interest, borrow against collateral, and manage their positions within the Solana-based lending protocol.

For information about the underlying blockchain architecture, see Blockchain Architecture.

For details about the frontend architecture and UI components, see Frontend Architecture. ## Feature Overview The platform provides five primary features implemented as React components with src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx377-839 src/components/withdraw/withdraw-data-access.tsx19-185 ## Markets and Banking System The markets system allows users to browse available lending pools and view their characteristics.

Each market represents a Bank account in the Solana program with specific parameters for lending and borrowing. ### Bank Data Structure The BankData interface defines the core properties of each lending market: ``` #mermaid-edb5qfwzuvf{font-family:ui-sans-serif,-apple-system,system-ui,Segoe UI,Helvetica;font-size:16px;fill:#333;}@keyframes ``` The useMarketsBanks() hook fetches bank data from the Solana program and calculates APY using the formula: ``` APY = (1 + r_period)^periodsPerYear - 1 ``` Where r_period = annualRate / periodsPerYear and periodsPerYear = SECONDS_IN_YEAR / interestAccrualPeriodSecs.

src/components/markets/markets-data-access.tsx175-211 ## Deposits and Interest Earning The deposits system enables users to deposit tokens into lending pools to earn interest.

## Borrowing and Lending

# Borrowing and Lending Relevant source files anchor/Anchor.toml src/components/borrow/active-borrow-positions.tsx src/components/borrow/borrow-data-access.tsx src/components/borrow/borrow-ui.tsx src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx src/components/deposits/deposits-ui.tsx src/components/repay/repay-ui.tsx This document covers the borrowing and lending functionality of the Stervault DeFi lending platform, including the process of borrowing tokens against deposited collateral, managing active borrow positions, and repaying loans.

The system enables users to leverage their crypto assets by borrowing against their deposits while maintaining proper risk management through loan-to-value (LTV) ratios and price oracle integration.

For information about depositing tokens that serve as collateral, see Deposits and Withdrawals.

For details about the underlying lending program structure, see Program Instructions. ## System Overview The borrowing and lending system allows users to create leveraged positions by borrowing tokens against their existing deposits.

The system maintains collateralized debt positions (CDPs) that are tracked on-chain and can be managed through the web interface. ### Core Borrowing Flow ``` Structures ### BorrowPositionData Interface The BorrowPositionData interface represents an active borrow position with comprehensive metadata for UI display and risk management. | Field | Type | Description | | --- | --- | --- | | publicKey | PublicKey | On-chain account address of the position | | owner | PublicKey | Wallet address of the position owner | | collateralMint | PublicKey | Token mint used as collateral | | borrowMint | PublicKey | Token mint being borrowed | | collaterals | number | Amount of collateral in s | | borroweds | number | Amount borrowed in s | | collateralAmount | number | Collateral amount in token units | | borrowAmount | number | Borrowed amount in token units | | collateralUsdValue | number | USD value of collateral | | borrowUsdValue | number | USD value src/components/borrow/active-borrow-positions.tsx1-533 ### Borrow Transaction Flow The borrow mutation in useBorrowTokens handles the complete borrowing process: 
* Input Validation: Validates borrowing parameters and checks token information 
* Price Feed Setup: Creates or validates PythNetworkFeedId accounts for both collateral and borrow tokens 
* Account Derivation: Derives necessary PDAs including position accounts 
* LTV Validation: Ensures the borrow amount doesn't exceed maximum LTV ratio 
* Transaction Execution: Calls the lending program's borrow instruction Sources: src/components/borrow/borrow-data-access.tsx254-579 ### Position ID Management The system determines position IDs by fetching the current user's global state and incrementing the position counter: ``` // Determine position ID from user global state let positionId = 1; const [userGlobalStatePDA] = PublicKey.findProgramAddressSync( [Buffer.from('user_global'), provider.publicKey.toBuffer()], program.programId, ); const userGlobalState = await program.account.userGlobalState.fetch(userGlobalStatePDA); positionId = userGlobalState.positions + 1; ``` Sources: src/components/borrow/borrow-data-access.tsx461-476 ## Position Management System ### Active Position Retrieval The useActiveBorrowPositions hook retrieves and processes all active borrow positions for a user: ``` src/components/borrow/borrow-data-access.tsx581-649 ### Maximum Borrow Amount Calculation The calculateMaxBorrowAmount function determines the maximum borrowable amount based on collateral value and LTV limits: ``` // Convert collateral to USD value const collateralUsdValue = collateralAmount * collateralPrice; // Calculate maximum borrow amount in USD const maxBorrowUsd = collateralUsdValue * maxLTV; // Convert max borrow USD back to token amount const maxBorrowAmount = maxBorrowUsd / borrowPrice; ``` Sources: src/components/borrow/borrow-data-access.tsx660-729 ### Health Factor Monitoring The system provides visual indicators for position health based on LTV ratios: 
* Green (< 50% of max LTV): Healthy position 
* Yellow (50-70% of max LTV): Moderate risk 
* Red (> 70% of max LTV): High liquidation risk Sources: src/components/borrow/active-borrow-positions.tsx115-137 src/components/deposits/deposits-ui.tsx174-195 ## Price Oracle Integration ### Pyth Price Feed Management The system integrates with Pyth Network for real-time price feeds through several mechanisms: 
* Price Feed ID Storage: Creates PythNetworkFeedId accounts for each token symbol 
* Account Address Resolution: Resolves price feed IDs to on-chain account addresses 
* Real-time Price Fetching: Uses useBatchPythPrices for efficient price updates Sources: src/components/borrow/borrow-data-access.tsx307-456 src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx131-175 ### src/components/borrow/borrow-data-access.tsx425-456 src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx140-175 ## UI Component Integration ### Form Validation and State Management The borrowing UI uses React Hook Form with Zod validation for robust input handling: ``` const borrowFormSchema = z.object({ borrowBankId: z.string().min(1, "Please select a token to borrow"), collateralBankId: z.string().min(1, "Please select a collateral"), amount: z.string().min(1, "Please enter an amount") .refine(val => !isNaN(parseFloat(val)), "Amount must be a number") .refine(val => parseFloat(val) > 0, "Amount must be greater than 0"), }); ``` Sources: src/components/borrow/borrow-ui.tsx63-70 ### Real-time LTV Updates The UI provides real-time LTV calculation updates as users modify borrow amounts: ``` // Calculate loan-to-value ratio when amount or selected banks change useEffect(() => { const borrowAmount = parseFloat(form.watch("amount")); const ltv = calculateLoanToValueRatio( borrowAmount, selectedCollateralDeposit, form.watch("borrowBankId"), selectedBorrowBank?.tokenInfo?.decimals ); setLoanToValue(ltv); }, [form.watch("amount"), selectedBorrowBank, selectedCollateralDeposit]); ``` Sources: src/components/borrow/borrow-ui.tsx165-194 ### Confirmation Flow The system implements a two-step confirmation process: 
* Form Submission: Validates inputs and shows confirmation screen 
* Transaction Execution: Confirms details and executes the blockchain transaction Sources: src/components/borrow/borrow-ui.tsx413-513 src/components/repay/repay-ui.tsx339-451

## Development Setup

# Development Setup Relevant source files anchor/scripts/clone-tokens.ts anchor/scripts/setup-pyth.ts anchor/tests/lending.spec.ts package.json yarn.lock This document provides an overview of the development environment setup for the Stervault DeFi lending platform.

It covers the core infrastructure components, scripts, and configuration required to build, test, and deploy the application across different environments (localnet, devnet, mainnet).

For detailed step-by-step environment setup instructions, see Local Environment Setup.

For testing strategies and test execution, see Testing.

For deployment processes and environment configuration, see Deployment and Configuration. ## Development Architecture Overview The development setup consists of three main layers: the Solana blockchain layer with Anchor framework, the Next.js frontend with React Query, and supporting infrastructure including AWS deployment and Pyth price feeds. ``` anchor/scripts/setup-pyth.ts1-217 anchor/scripts/clone-tokens.ts1-268 ## Environment Configuration The platform supports multiple deployment environments with different configurations for RPC endpoints, program IDs, and external service integrations. | Environment | RPC Endpoint | Purpose | Configuration | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | localnet | http://localhost:8899 | Local development and testing | Uses solana-test-validator with cloned accounts | | devnet | https://api.devnet.solana.com | Integration testing | Live Solana devnet with test tokens | | mainnet | https://api.mainnet-beta.solana.com | Production deployment | Live mainnet with real assets | The environment-specific configurations are managed through: * .env.devnet for devnet settings * .env.custom for custom configurations 
* Environment variable injection via env-cmd package Sources: package.json17-18 anchor/scripts/setup-pyth.ts8-11 anchor/scripts/clone-tokens.ts14-23 ## Core Setup Components ### Anchor Program Development The Solana program development uses the Anchor framework with comprehensive testing through anchor-bankrun.

anchor/tests/lending.spec.ts34-51 anchor/tests/lending.spec.ts85-131 ### Price Feed Integration The Pyth Network integration requires setting up price feed accounts for asset valuation.

The setup-pyth.ts script handles fetching price feed data and creating environment-specific configurations. ### Supported Price Feeds: * *
* 

**SOL:**
** 0xef0d8b6fda2ceba41da15d4095d1da392a0d2f8ed0c6c7bc0f4cfac8c280b56d * *
* 

**USDC:**
** 0xeaa020c61cc479712813461ce153894a96a6c00b21ed0cfc2798d1f9a9e9c94a * *
* 

**USDT:**
** 0x2b89b9dc8fdf9f34709a5b106b472f0f39bb6ca9ce04b0fd7f2e971688e2e53b 
* And additional tokens including TRUMP, JLP, JitoSOL, JUP, WBTC, ETH, Fartcoin Sources: anchor/scripts/setup-pyth.ts20-32 anchor/scripts/setup-pyth.ts92-108 ### Token Creation and Metadata The clone-tokens.ts script creates SPL tokens with Metaplex metadata for testing and development.

anchor/scripts/clone-tokens.ts188-213 anchor/scripts/clone-tokens.ts222-226 ## Development Workflow The typical development workflow involves sequential setup of external dependencies, local blockchain infrastructure, and application components.

Initial Setup: 
* Run setup-pyth.ts to configure Pyth price feeds 
* Execute clone-tokens.ts to create test tokens 
* Start local validator with anchor-localnet 
* Run integration tests with anchor-test Active Development: 
* Use dev for local frontend development 
* Use dev:devnet for testing against live devnet 
* Deploy with aws-deploy for production releases The setup scripts create reusable configuration files in the price-feeds/ and keys/tokens/ directories that persist across development sessions.

Sources: anchor/scripts/setup-pyth.ts110-115 anchor/scripts/clone-tokens.ts245-246 package.json6-12

## Markets and Banking

# Markets and Banking Relevant source files next.config.mjs src/app/deposit-tokens/page.tsx src/components/add-bank/add-bank-data-access.tsx src/components/add-bank/add-bank-ui.tsx src/components/bank-deposits/bank-deposits-ui.tsx src/components/deposit-tokens/deposit-tokens-ui.tsx src/components/markets/markets-data-access.tsx src/components/markets/markets-ui.tsx src/components/onboarding/onboarding-ui.tsx This document covers the markets and banking system within Stervault, which manages token banks (lending pools), displays market data, and provides interfaces for bank creation and interaction.

This system serves as the primary interface for users to discover available lending markets and administrators to create new token banks.

For information about the actual deposit and withdrawal processes, see Deposits and Withdrawals.

For borrowing mechanics, see Borrowing and Lending.

For the underlying Solana program structure, see Lending Program Overview. ## Bank Account Structure The banking system revolves around Bank accounts stored on the Solana blockchain.

Each bank represents a lending pool for a specific SPL token. ``` #mermaid-82qmn1ch5a{font-family:ui-sans-serif,-apple-system,system-ui,Segoe UI,Helvetica;font-size:16px;fill:#333;}@keyframes ms",verdana,arial,sans-serif;}useMarketsBanks()getLendingProgram()program.account.bank.all()RawBankAccount[]bnToNumber()calculateApy()fetch token metadataBankData[]React Query cache ``` The data processing pipeline includes: 
* Raw Data Fetch: program.account.bank.all() retrieves all bank accounts 
* BN Conversion: bnToNumber() converts Anchor BN types to JavaScript numbers 
* APY Calculation: calculateApy() computes annual percentage yield using the formula: APY = (1 + r_period)^periodsPerYear - 1 
* Token Metadata: Fetched from /tokens_${SOLANA_ENV}.json files 
* Caching: Results cached via React Query with 30-second refetch interval Sources: src/components/markets/markets-data-access.tsx118-241 src/components/markets/markets-data-access.tsx95-116 ## Market Parameter | Frontend Type | Program Type | Conversion | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | liquidationThreshold | number (%) | BN (basis points) | * 100 | | maxLtv | number (%) | BN (basis points) | * 100 | | depositInterestRate | number (%) | BN (basis points) | * 100 | | minDeposit | number | BN | * 10^decimals | | depositFee | number (%) | BN (basis points) | * 100 | The system performs token validation and calculates required Program Derived Addresses (PDAs): ``` // Bank PDA calculation const [bankPDA] = PublicKey.findProgramAddressSync( [tokenMint.toBuffer()], programId ); // Bank treasury PDA calculation const [bankTokenAccountPDA] = PublicKey.findProgramAddressSync( [Buffer.from('treasury'), tokenMint.toBuffer()], programId ); ``` Sources: * Price Feed Mapping: Token symbols mapped to Pyth feed IDs via priceFeedIds constant 
* Batch Price Fetch: useBatchPythPrices() retrieves current prices for all active feeds 
* Amount Conversion: const tokenAmount = amount / Math.pow(10, decimals); const usdValue = tokenAmount * priceData.price; 
* Market Calculations: Total TVL, utilization rates, and statistics computed from USD values Sources: src/components/bank-deposits/bank-deposits-ui.tsx148-201 * Real-time Updates: 30-second refresh intervals for market data 
* Price Accuracy: Integration with Pyth Network oracles 
* Performance: React Query caching minimizes blockchain calls 
* User Experience: Responsive UI with loading states and error handling Sources: src/components/markets/markets-data-access.tsx118-241 src/components/bank-deposits/bank-deposits-ui.tsx104-201 src/components/add-bank/add-bank-data-access.tsx21-57

## Deposits and Withdrawals

# Deposits and Withdrawals Relevant source files src/app/withdraw/layout.tsx src/app/withdraw/page.tsx src/components/borrow/active-borrow-positions.tsx src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx src/components/deposits/deposits-ui.tsx src/components/repay/repay-ui.tsx src/components/withdraw/withdraw-data-access.tsx src/components/withdraw/withdraw-ui.tsx This document covers the deposits and withdrawals functionality in the Stervault lending platform, including user interface components, data access patterns, and blockchain integration.

This system allows users to deposit tokens into lending pools to earn interest and withdraw their deposited tokens along with accrued earnings.

For information about borrowing against deposits as collateral, see Borrowing and Lending.

For details about the underlying markets and banking system, see Markets and Banking. ## System Architecture The deposits and withdrawals system consists of several layers that work together to provide a seamless user experience while maintaining secure blockchain operations. ### Component Architecture ``` #mermaid-3rbaoybndi7{font-family:ui-sans-serif,-apple-system,system-ui,Segoe src/components/withdraw/withdraw-data-access.tsx75-165 ## Core Data Structures The system uses several key interfaces to represent user positions and account states. ### UserDeposit Interface The UserDeposit interface represents a user's deposit in a specific lending pool: | Field | Type | Description | | --- | --- | --- | | publicKey | PublicKey | The deposit account's public key | | bankPublicKey | PublicKey | The associated bank's public key | | mintAddress | PublicKey | The token mint address | | depositAmount | number | Amount of tokens deposited | | deposits | number | s representing the deposit | | lastUpdateTime | number | Last update timestamp | | usdValue | number? | USD value of the deposit | | priceData | PythPriceData? | Real-time price information | Sources: src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx89-113 ### BorrowPositionData Interface The BorrowPositionData interface represents an active borrowing position: | Field | Type | Description | | --- | --- | --- | | publicKey | PublicKey | Position account public key | | collateralMint | PublicKey | Collateral token mint | | borrowMint | PublicKey | Borrowed token mint | | collateralAmount | number | Amount of collateral | | borrowAmount | number | Amount borrowed | | ltvRatio | number | Loan-to-value ratio percentage | Sources: src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx40-67 ## Deposit keyprogram.account.userTokenState.all()Process each accountExtract mint address from _bnGet token metadataCalculate USD valuesFilter deposits > 0Return UserDeposit[]useBatchPythPrices() ``` The deposit fetching process handles several complex data transformations: 
* Account Processing: Retrieves all UserTokenState accounts for the connected wallet 
* Mint Address Extraction: Extracts token mint addresses from BN (Big Number) objects stored in _bn fields 
* Token Metadata Lookup: Maps mint addresses to token metadata including symbols, logos, and price feed IDs 
* Price Integration: Fetches real-time prices from Pyth Network using batch requests 
* USD Value Calculation: Combines token amounts with current prices for USD valuations Sources: src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx408-754 ### Deposit Display Components The deposits UI * Summary Statistics: Total value, active deposits count, and token diversity 
* Expandable Deposit Cards: Detailed view with earnings calculations and token information 
* USD/Token Toggle: Switch between displaying values in USD or native token amounts 
* Real-time Price Integration: Live price updates from Pyth Network 
* Borrow Position Integration: Shows related borrowing activities Sources: src/components/deposits/deposits-ui.tsx50-446 ## Withdrawal Operations ### Withdrawal Data Access The useWithdraw hook process involves several security and validation steps: 
* Deposit Validation: Confirms the user owns the specified deposit 
* Mint Information: Retrieves token decimals and validation data 
* PDA Calculation: Derives the correct Program Derived Address for the user account 
* Token Account Resolution: Finds or prepares the user's associated token account 
* Transaction Execution: Calls the Solana program's withdraw instruction Sources: src/components/withdraw/withdraw-data-access.tsx31-44 src/components/withdraw/withdraw-data-access.tsx75-165 ### * URL-based Navigation: Accepts bankId and mintAddress parameters to identify specific deposits 
* Input Validation: Prevents withdrawals exceeding deposit balance or invalid amounts 
* MAX Button: Automatically fills the maximum withdrawable amount 
* Real-time USD Conversion: Shows USD equivalent of withdrawal amount 
* Transaction Feedback: Toast notifications and loading states during processing Sources: src/components/withdraw/withdraw-ui.tsx32-416 ## Blockchain Integration ### Program Accounts and PDAs The ### Transaction Structure Withdrawal transactions require specific account configurations: | Account | Type | Description | | --- | --- | --- | | signer | Signer | User's wallet public key | | mint | Info | Token mint account | | bank | Info | Bank account for the token | | bankTokenAccount | Mut | Bank's token treasury (PDA) | | userAccount | Mut | User's deposit state (PDA) | | userTokenAccount | Mut | User's associated token account | | tokenProgram | Program | SPL Token program | | systemProgram | Program | System program for account creation | Sources: prefixValidate 64-char hexuseBatchPythPrices()Real-time price dataCalculate deposit USD valuesDisplay in UISummary statistics ``` Price feed integration includes: 
* Feed ID Validation: Ensures price feed IDs are valid 64-character hex strings 
* Batch Processing: Optimizes API calls by requesting multiple prices simultaneously 
* Fallback Pricing: Uses default prices for common tokens when Pyth data is unavailable 
* Real-time Updates: Automatically refreshes deposit values when new price data arrives Sources: src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx15-16 src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx662-702 src/components/deposits/deposits-data-access.tsx757-826

## Transaction History

# Transaction History Relevant source files src/app/transaction-history/layout.tsx src/app/transaction-history/page.tsx src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-ui.tsx src/components/ui/table.tsx The transaction history system provides users with a comprehensive view of their lending protocol interactions on the Solana blockchain.

This system fetches, processes, and displays transaction data including deposits, withdrawals, borrows, and repayments with advanced filtering, , and export capabilities.

For information about the underlying blockchain transactions and program instructions, see Program Instructions. ## System Architecture The transaction history system follows a layered architecture MissReturn Cached DataFetch from RPCExponential BackoffStore in Cache15min TTLPeriodic Cleanup ``` Key performance features: 
* Transaction Cache: 15-minute TTL with cleanup at src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx22-24 
* Request Throttling: 30-second minimum between fetches at src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx226-232 
* Exponential Backoff: Rate limit handling at src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx30-53 
* Batch Processing: Limited to 10 transactions per fetch at src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx24 
* Lazy Loading: Details fetched on demand at src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-ui.tsx187-216 ### Rate Limiting Protection The system uses withExponentialBackoff function to handle RPC rate limits with progressive delays and retry logic, ensuring reliable data fetching even under high load conditions.

Sources: src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx21-29 src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx30-53 src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx376-385 ## Transaction Data Structure The core data structure TransactionHistoryItem represents processed transaction information with optional lazy-loaded details. ``` interface TransactionHistoryItem { id: string; // Transaction signature type: TransactionType; // Classified transaction type timestamp: number; // Block time in milliseconds amount?: number; // Token amount (lazy loaded) token?: TokenInfo; // Token metadata (lazy loaded) status: 'success' | 'error' | 'pending'; fee?: number; // Transaction fee in SOL blockTime?: number; // Raw block time slot?: number; // Solana slot number } ``` Token amount extraction uses pre/post token balance arrays from transaction metadata, avoiding additional RPC calls for mint information at src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx133-201 Sources: src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx67-85 src/components/transaction-history/transaction-history-data-access.tsx133-201

## Local Environment Setup

# Local Environment Setup Relevant source files anchor/scripts/clone-tokens.ts anchor/scripts/setup-pyth.ts src/app/layout.tsx src/components/dashboard/dashboard-data-access.tsx src/components/dashboard/dashboard-ui.tsx src/components/faucet/faucet-data-access.tsx src/components/faucet/faucet-ui.tsx src/lib/connection-health.ts src/lib/request-batcher.ts This document provides a comprehensive guide for setting up a local development environment for the Stervault DeFi lending platform.

It covers the configuration of a local Solana validator, Pyth price oracle setup, test token creation, and frontend development server initialization.

This guide focuses specifically on local development setup.

For deployment to devnet or mainnet environments, see Deployment and Configuration.

For testing procedures after setup, see Testing. ## Environment Overview The local development environment consists of several interconnected components that work together to provide a complete DeFi Content ``` Sources: src/app/layout.tsx11-29 ### Environment Detection The application automatically detects the environment and loads appropriate token lists: ``` // Environment detection logic let env: string; if (cluster?.network === 'devnet' || cluster?.name === 'devnet') { env = 'devnet'; } else if (cluster?.name === 'local' || process.env.

NEXT_PUBLIC_SOLANA_ENV === 'localnet') { env = 'localnet'; } else { env = process.env.

NEXT_PUBLIC_SOLANA_ENV || 'localnet'; } ``` Sources: src/components/faucet/faucet-data-access.tsx197-204 ### Token Faucet Integration The faucet system provides test tokens through the /api/faucet endpoint, loading tokens from the generated mapping: ``` const tokensResponse = await fetch(`/tokens_${env}.json`); const availableTokens = tokensResponse.ok ? await tokensResponse.json() : []; ``` Sources: src/components/faucet/faucet-data-access.tsx206-207 ## Complete Setup Process ### Step-by 
- Step Setup 
* Install Dependencies npm install cd anchor && npm install 
* Setup Pyth Price Feeds cd anchor npm run setup-pyth -- --env localnet 
* Create Test Tokens npm run clone-tokens -- --env localnet 
* Start Local Validator with Pyth Accounts bash ./price-feeds/restart_with_pyth.sh 
* Start Frontend Application cd .. npm run dev ### Restart Script Functionality The generated restart_with_pyth.sh script performs these operations: 
* Stops existing validator: pkill solana-test-validator 
* Dumps required programs from devnet 
* Starts validator with cloned accounts and programs 
* Configures Solana CLI to use local endpoint Sources: anchor/scripts/setup-pyth.ts123-174 ## Verification ### Connection Health Monitoring The application includes connection health monitoring to ensure proper validator connectivity: ``` interface ConnectionHealth { isHealthy: boolean; latency: number; lastCheck: Date; errorCount: number; status: 'healthy' | 'degraded' | 'unhealthy'; } ``` Sources: src/lib/connection-health.ts3-9 ### Verification Checklist 
* Validator Status: Confirm solana-test-validator is running on port 8899 
* Pyth Feeds: Verify price feed accounts are accessible 
* Test Tokens: Confirm tokens appear in wallet and faucet 
* Frontend: Check dashboard loads without errors 
* Faucet: Test token claiming functionality ### Health Check Endpoints The connection monitor performs regular health checks: 
* Endpoint: getLatestBlockhash('confirmed') 
* Interval: 30 seconds 
* Thresholds: 2 second latency, 5 error maximum Sources: src/lib/connection-health.ts29-34 src/lib/connection-health.ts22-23 ## Troubleshooting ### Common Issues | Issue | Cause | Solution | | --- | --- | --- | | Validator won't start | Port 8899 in use | pkill solana-test-validator | | Price feeds not working | Missing Pyth setup | Re-run setup-pyth.ts | | No test tokens | Token creation failed | Check signer balance, re-run clone-tokens.ts | | Faucet errors | Missing token mapping | Verify tokens_localnet.json exists | ### Rate Limiting Handling The application includes request batching and rate limiting protection: ``` class RequestBatcher<T> { private config: BatchConfig = { maxBatchSize: 10, maxWaitTime: 100, // 100ms max wait minWaitTime: 10 // 10ms min wait } } ``` Sources: src/lib/request-batcher.ts25-29 ### Cache Management A TTL cache system reduces redundant RPC calls: 
* Default *
* 

**TTL:**
** 5 minutes 
* Cleanup Interval: 5 minutes 
* Auto-expiry: Removes stale entries Sources: src/lib/request-batcher.ts158 src/lib/request-batcher.ts207-209

## Deployment and Configuration

# Deployment and Configuration Relevant source files package.json public/tokens_devnet.json src/app/api/faucet/route.ts src/components/cluster/cluster-data-access.tsx yarn.lock This document covers the deployment strategies and configuration management for the Stervault DeFi lending platform.

It details how to deploy the application to different environments, configure blockchain networks, manage environment variables, and set up supporting infrastructure components.

For information about setting up the local development environment, see Local Environment Setup.

For testing procedures and strategies, see Testing. ## Environment Configuration The platform supports multiple deployment environments with different blockchain networks.

The environment configuration is primarily managed through the NEXT_PUBLIC_SOLANA_ENV environment variable and corresponding cluster configurations. ### Supported Environments | Environment | Network | RPC Endpoint | Token Config | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | localnet | Local Solana Validator | http://localhost:8899 | tokens_localnet.json | | devnet | Solana Devnet | clusterApiUrl('devnet') | tokens_devnet.json | | mainnet | Solana Mainnet | Custom endpoint required | tokens_mainnet.json | The cluster configuration is managed in src/components/cluster/cluster-data-access.tsx26-37 where defaultClusters are determined based on the environment: ``` export const defaultClusters: Cluster[] = process.env.

NEXT_PUBLIC_SOLANA_ENV === 'localnet' ? [{ name: 'local', endpoint: 'http://localhost:8899' }] : [{ name: 'devnet', endpoint: clusterApiUrl('devnet'), network: ClusterNetwork.

Devnet }] ``` ### Environment Configuration Flow ``` #mermaid-iez1wocafns{font-family:ui-sans-serif,-apple-system,system-ui,Segoe UI,Helvetica;font-size:16px;fill:#333;}@keyframes frontend supports multiple deployment strategies with environment-specific configurations. ### Build and Deployment Scripts The deployment process is orchestrated through npm scripts defined in package.json5-19: * build: Standard Next.js production build * start: Start production server * dev:devnet: Development mode with devnet configuration * dev:custom: Development mode with custom environment * aws-deploy: Deploy to AWS infrastructure * aws-sync: Synchronize with AWS deployment ### AWS Deployment Pipeline ``` Sources: package.json6-10 ## Configuration Management ### Token Configuration Token configurations are environment-specific and stored as JSON files in the public directory.

The devnet configuration is shown in public/tokens_devnet.json1-244 with token metadata including: 
* Contract addresses 
* Decimals and symbols 
* Pyth price feed IDs 
* Token metadata (logos, names) ### Faucet Configuration The development faucet service configuration is managed in src/app/api/faucet/route.ts7-22 with environment-based settings: ``` function loadSignerKeypair(): Keypair { const privateKeyEnv = process.env.

FAUCET_SIGNER_PRIVATE_KEY; if (privateKeyEnv) { const secretKey = Uint8Array.from(JSON.parse(privateKeyEnv)); return Keypair.fromSecretKey(secretKey); } } ``` ### Configuration Loading Flow ```
